\mu_{0}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
, also
\mu_{t}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
at any time
t>0
and

\displaystyle\|\mu_{t}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

(3.0.5)

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}.

Moreover we have:

•

if
\mu_{0}\geq 0
then
\mu_{t}\geq 0
at any time
t>0
,

•

if
\mu_{0}
does not depend on
x
or
(x,\theta)
neither
\mu_{t}
does at any time
t>0
, and the same properties hold without integrating with respect to
x
or
(x,\theta)
.

Before proving Proposition
3.1
we recall that the existence and the uniqueness of the mild solutions have already been proved in
[
7
,
14
]
, as well as the fact that the
L^{1}
distance between two solutions is not increasing in time. Nevertheless, for self consistency of the paper we prove it, both because our proof is slightly different and because the main Lemma we need to prove it will be also useful for other results in this paper.

Notice also that the quantity in (
3.0.5
) ensures that
\mu_{t}\in L^{p}
, since for any
p\in[1,+\infty]
using Hölder inequality we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq(4\pi t)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,t]\times[-1,1])}.

Of course, a priori this estimate does not ensure that the family
\{\mu_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is bounded in
L^{p}
if
p\neq 1
.

Finally, we point out that if
\mu_{0}
does not depend on
x
(respectively on
(x,\theta)
), the second summand in both (
3.0.4
) and (
3.0.5
) can be expressed as
\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,t]\times[-1,1])}
(respectively as
\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}([0,t]\times[-1,1])}
).

3.0.1.
An intermediate step to prove Proposition
3.1
.

To prove Proposition
3.1
we need an intermediate result. If we look at the equation (
1.1.9
) and we evaluate
\mu_{t}
for
s=0
, we get an equation that involves only
\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s=0,h)
and
\mu_{0}
, that is

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(x,\theta,0,h)

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}(x-v(\theta)t,\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{t^{\prime}}(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),0,h^{\prime}).

Therefore the crucial point is proving the existence and the uniqueness of
\mu_{t}(x,\theta,0,h)
, since it implies the ones of
\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)
by (
1.1.9
).

Therefore we first prove the following Lemma, where
\mu_{t}(x,\theta,0,h)
is replaced by
\rho(x,\theta,t,h)
and
\mu_{0}(x,\theta,t,h)
is replaced by
\mu(x,\theta,t,h)
. We write it in a more general way, since we will need it in a slightly different formulation.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
or
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{T}^{2}=\mathbb{R}^{2}/\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,
T>0
,
p\in[1,+\infty]
and
\mu\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])
. Then there exists a unique
\rho\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])
such that

\displaystyle\rho(x,\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle=\mu(x-tv(\theta),\theta,t,h)

(3.0.6)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\rho(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

Moreover for any
t\leq T
we have

(3.0.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\rho(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h)|E(t-t^{\prime},h)\leq\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h)|,

(3.0.8)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\rho(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h)E(t-t^{\prime},h)=\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\mu(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h).

Moreover, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\rho(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\|\mu(\cdot,\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}

(3.0.9)

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}.

Furthermore, the following properties hold:

•

if
\mu\geq 0
then
\rho\geq 0
,

•

if
\mu
does not depend on
x
or
(x,\theta)
neither does
\rho
. In such case, (
3.0.7
) and (
3.0.8
) hold also without integrating with respect to
x
or
(x,\theta)
.

Before proving the Lemma, we point out that if
\mu
does not depend on
x
or
(x,\theta)
, (
3.0.9
) can be expressed by substituting the norm
\|\mu(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}
with the modulus
|\mu(\theta,s,h)|
or
|\mu(s,h)|
.

Proof.

Since the proof technique does not depend on whenever
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
or
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
p\in[1,+\infty]
, we prove this result only in the case
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
p\in[1,+\infty)
.

To prove the existence of such
\rho
in the equation (
3.0.6
) we use the Banach fixed-point Theorem being careful that the integral of the function
Q(t,h|h^{\prime})
in the domain
\{(t,h):0\leq t\leq T,h\in[-1,1]\}
is exactly 1 (and not
<1
) as soon as
T\geq\frac{1}{1-|h^{\prime}|}
(see (
2.2.1
)). Therefore, splitting
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]
into
\left\{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{k}{2},\frac{k+1}{2})\times[-1,1]\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}^{k}:=\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times\left[\frac{k}{2},\frac{k+1}{2}\wedge T\right)\times[-1,1],

and we find step by step
\rho
as a function
L^{1}({\mathcal{M}}^{k})
, for
k=0,1,\dots,\lfloor 2T\rfloor
, going on by induction on
k
. We only prove the inductive step because it has no substantial differences from the basic step
k=0
. Therefore, for
t\in[\frac{k}{2},\frac{k+1}{2})
, we write the equation (
3.0.6
) splitting the integral with respect to
t^{\prime}
in two summands

\displaystyle\rho(x,\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle=\mu(x-tv(\theta),\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\frac{k}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\rho(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{\frac{k}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\rho(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

that is, supposing that we have already defined
\bar{\rho}
as the solution of (
3.0.6
) for any
t\in[0,\frac{k}{2})
, we denote

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle:L^{p}({\mathcal{M}}^{k})\to L^{p}({\mathcal{M}}^{k})

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}[\rho](x,\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle:=\mu(x-tv(\theta),\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{k/2}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\bar{\rho}(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{k/2}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\rho(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

This map preserves the periodicity in
\theta
and therefore it makes sense for
\theta\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}
. It preserves also the periodicity with respect to
x
, and therefore the argument works also when
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{T}^{2}
.

{\mathcal{F}}
is a contraction with respect to the canonical norm associated with
L^{p}({\mathcal{M}}^{k})
. Indeed by Jensen’s inequality (
4.2.8
), one has

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{F}}[\rho]-{\mathcal{F}}[\rho^{\prime}]\|_{L^{p}({\mathcal{M}}^{k})}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{\frac{k}{2}}^{\frac{k+1}{2}\wedge T}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{k/2}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})(\rho-\rho^{\prime})(x^{\prime},\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|^{p},

with

x^{\prime}:=x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\textrm{ and }\theta^{\prime}:=\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}).

Therefore

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{F}}[\rho]-{\mathcal{F}}[\rho^{\prime}]\|_{L^{p}({\mathcal{M}}^{k})}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{\frac{k}{2}}^{\frac{k+1}{2}\wedge T}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{k/2}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})(\rho-\rho^{\prime})(x^{\prime},\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{\frac{k}{2}}^{\frac{k+1}{2}\wedge T}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{k/2}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\rho-\rho^{\prime}|^{p}(x^{\prime},\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\xi_{p},

with

\xi_{p}=\xi_{p}(t,h)=(1-E(t-\frac{k}{2},h))^{p-1}.

Now if one defines

\displaystyle C_{E}:=\min\left\{E(t,h):t\in\left[0,\frac{1}{2}\right],h\in[-1,1]\right\}>0,

in such a way to get by the previous computations

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{F}}[\rho]-{\mathcal{F}}[\rho^{\prime}]\|_{L^{p}({\mathcal{M}}^{k})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq(1-C_{E})^{p-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{\frac{k}{2}}^{\frac{k+1}{2}\wedge T}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\rho-\rho^{\prime}|^{p}(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\int_{t^{\prime}}^{\frac{k+1}{2}\wedge T}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(1-C_{E})^{p-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{\frac{k}{2}}^{\frac{k+1}{2}\wedge T}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\rho-\rho^{\prime}|^{p}(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\left(1-E\left(\frac{k+1}{2}\wedge T-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq(1-C_{E})^{p}\|\rho-\rho^{\prime}\|_{L^{p}({\mathcal{M}}^{k})}^{p},

one can use the Banach fixed-point Theorem on
\mathcal{F}
to extend
\rho
to
{\mathcal{M}}^{k}
.

The uniqueness is also a direct consequence of the Banach fixed-point Theorem applied step by step to
L^{p}({\mathcal{M}}^{k})
.

To prove now inequality (
3.0.7
) we simply apply the triangle inequality in the integral defining
\rho
in (
3.0.6
) and for any
t\leq T
we get

\displaystyle|\rho(x,\theta,t,h)|

\displaystyle\leq|\mu(x-tv(\theta),\theta,t,h)|

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\rho(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|,

Therefore, we integrate both sides of the above inequality with respect to
(x,\theta,t,h)
. In this way, in the right-hand side one can change variables

x-tv(\theta)\to x,

in the first summand and

x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta)\to x,\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})\to\theta,

in the second one, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\rho(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\mu(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h)\right|

(3.0.10)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\rho(x,\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime})|.

Since the second summand (
3.0.10
) in the right-hand side of the above inequality can also be written as

(
3.0.10
)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\rho(x,\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime})|\int_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\rho(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|(1-E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})),

the term in the left-hand side of the inequality containing (
3.0.10
) gets deleted and we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\rho(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h)|E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h)|\hskip 7.3612pt\forall t\leq T.

The same steps, without the modulus and therefore without the triangle inequality, prove property (
3.0.8
).

Before proving (
3.0.9
), we focus on the last statement of the Theorem:
\mu\geq 0
implies
\rho\geq 0
because the map
{\mathcal{F}}
preserves also the positivity of the argument and the metric space of non negative
L^{1}
functions is complete with respect to
L^{1}
norm, therefore the Banach fixed-point Theorem can be applied in this space. The same holds for the dependance on
x
and on
(x,\theta)
, where the same argument works.

We finally have to prove (
3.0.9
). To this purpose, we split
\rho
in the sum of three contributions, that is

\displaystyle\rho(x,\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle=\beta(x,\theta,t,h)+\mu(x-tv(\theta),\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta)-t^{\prime}v(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

with
\beta
satisfying

\displaystyle\beta(x,\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\mu(x^{\prime},\theta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(3.0.12)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\beta(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

and where

x^{\prime}:=x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta)-(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime})v(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}))-t^{\prime\prime}v(\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime})),

and

\theta^{\prime\prime}:=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}).

By applying Minkowski’s inequality to equation (
), one gets

\displaystyle\|\rho(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\mu(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(3.0.13)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\|\mu(\cdot,\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

and therefore we can conclude the proof if we estimate
\|\beta(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
.

Step 1:
we want to prove that if

\displaystyle G(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime}),

with
f
as in Definition
1.8
, then

\displaystyle\|\beta(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(3.0.14)

\displaystyle\leq G(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})G(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(3.0.15)

\displaystyle+2\frac{c}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}G(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|\|\beta(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

To get this, we first shorten

F(\theta,t,h):=\|\beta(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

and then we notice that by using Minkowski’s inequality in the definition of
\beta
(
3.0.12
), changing then the integration order and shortening again

\theta^{\prime\prime}:=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),

we get

\displaystyle F(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\beta(\cdot,\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\|\beta(\cdot,\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(3.0.16)

\displaystyle=G(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})F(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

Now we argue as in the proof of Lemma
4.2
: first notice that by iterating twice over (
3.0.16
) we get

\displaystyle F(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle\leq G(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})G(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(3.0.17)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})F(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

with
f
as in Definition
1.8
. Notice also that with the same steps as Lemma
4.2
, that is, integrating both sides of (
3.0.17
) over
\theta\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi},t\in[0,T],h\in[-1,1]
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dhF(\theta,t,h)E^{(2)}(T-t,h)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dhG(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}G(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\underbrace{\int_{t^{\prime}}^{T}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})}_{\leq 1}

(3.0.18)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dhG(\theta,t,h).

Now if we multiply both the sides of (
3.0.18
) by
\frac{c}{2\pi}
, with
c\in(0,1)
, and if we subtract and add back the left-hand side into (
3.0.17
), we get
Step 1
.

Step 2:
to estimate
F
, we begin with estimating all the three terms concerning
G
in (
3.0.14
) and (
3.0.15
).

Notice that by definition

\displaystyle G(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\underbrace{f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})}_{\leq C\textrm{ by \eqref{deceffe} of Lemma \ref{propreffe}}}

(3.0.19)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

and therefore also the second one has a very similar bound, indeed

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\underbrace{G(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{\leq C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\textrm{ by \eqref{normaLprhostep6}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})}_{\leq 1}

(3.0.20)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

That is, by (
3.0.19
) and (
3.0.20
), we get

(3.0.21)

\displaystyle\eqref{normaLpcorrezioni1}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Finally, (
3.0.15
) can be bounded as

(
3.0.15
)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})}_{\leq 1\textrm{ by \eqref{fint1} of Lemma \ref{propreffe}}}

(3.0.22)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Therefore, by substituting (
3.0.21
) and (
3.0.22
) into
Step 1
, we can write

\displaystyle F(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(3.0.23)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|F(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

Step 3:
we prove here that, up to another constant
C^{\prime}>0
, the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\|\beta(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle=F(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\hskip 7.3612pt\forall(\theta,t,h)\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1].

To this purpose, also define

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(\theta,t,h):=\frac{F(\theta,t,h)}{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}},

and notice that the quantity above is well-defined. Indeed, since if the denominator is zero, then the same holds for the numerator. To prove this claim, suppose that
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{T}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=0
, then by (
3.0.23
) we have

F(\theta,t,h)\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|F(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),\hskip 8.19447pt\forall t\leq T.

Therefore denoting, for
c
small enough,

d:=\sup_{h\in[-1,1]}\left\|f(\cdot,\cdot,h|\cdot)-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}<1,

the constant provided by Lemma
4.3
, which we will prove Section
4
, if
t\leq T
one gets

\displaystyle F(\theta,t,h)\leq\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|,

then

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}\leq d\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])},

and therefore

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}=0\textrm{ since }d<1.

Now that we know that
\tilde{F}
is finite, by noticing that the denominator in the definition of
\tilde{F}
is increasing with respect to
t
, dividing both sides of (
3.0.23
) by
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
, we get

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(\theta,t,h)\leq C+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|\tilde{F}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

Now let
T>0
: if
t\leq T
, using again
d:=\sup_{h\in[-1,1]}\|f(\cdot,\cdot,h|\cdot)-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}\|_{L^{1}}<1
provided by Lemma
4.3
, by the previous inequality we have

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle\leq C+\|\tilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C+\|\tilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}d,

then

\|\tilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}\leq C+\|\tilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}d,

and therefore

\|\tilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}\leq\frac{C}{1-d},\hskip 9.24994pt\forall T>0,\textrm{ that is, }\|\tilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\leq\frac{C}{1-d}.

Hence, by the definition of
F
and
\tilde{F}
, one gets

\|\beta(\cdot,\theta,t,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=F(\theta,t,h)\leq\frac{C}{1-d}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

that is the content of
Step 3
. Combining the previous estimate with (
3.0.13
) we can conclude the proof of (
3.0.9
).
∎

When studying the dependance on
x
, a slightly different version of Lemma
3.1
is needed:

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{R}^{2},k\neq(0,0)
,
T>0
,
p\in[1,+\infty]
and
\mu\in L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1];\mathbb{C})
. Then there exists a unique
\rho\in L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1];\mathbb{C})
such that

\displaystyle\rho(\theta,t,h)=\mu(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\rho(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

3.0.2.
Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

Proof.

We start by proving the existence and the uniqueness: Lemma
3.1
with
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
or
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
p\in[1,+\infty]
and
\mu=\mu_{0}
provides the existence of a function
\rho
satisfying (
3.0.6
). Therefore, if we define
\mu_{t}(x,\theta,0,h)=\rho(x,\theta,t,h)
, and we then use equation (
1.1.9
) to obtain
\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)
, we get a mild solution of the equation (
1.1.2
). The uniqueness is a consequence of the fact that the relation (
1.1.9
) forces
\mu_{t}(x,\theta,0,h)
to be a solution of (
3.0.6
), which is unique thanks to Lemma
3.1
, and therefore also
\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)
is uniquely defined for any
s\geq 0
.

The next step is to prove property (
3.0.1
).

By representation formula (
1.1.9
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{0}(x-tv(\theta),\theta,s+t,h)|

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\mu_{t^{\prime}}(x-(t-t^{\prime})v(\theta),\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),0,h^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{t}^{t+T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{0}(x,\theta,s,h)|

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\mu_{t^{\prime}}(x,\theta,0,h^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{t}^{t+T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{0}(x,\theta,s,h)|

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\mu_{t^{\prime}}(x,\theta,0,h^{\prime})|\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime}).

Now we look at the last term, and since

\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})=E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})-E(t+T-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\leq E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

we can write

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{t}^{t+T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{0}(x,\theta,s,h)|

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\mu_{t^{\prime}}(x,\theta,0,h^{\prime})|E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{\leq\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\mu_{0}(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|\textrm{ by }\eqref{propr1}\textrm{ of Lemma \ref{lemma:rho}}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{t}^{t+T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{0}(x,\theta,s,h)|

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\mu_{0}(x,\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{X}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t+T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{0}(x,\theta,s,h)|,

and this proves property (
3.0.1
).

As for (
3.0.2
), it is sufficient to take the limit
T\to+\infty
in the equation (
3.0.1
).

Then, to prove property (
3.0.3
), the same argument as the one used to prove property (
3.0.1
) works: the only differences are that (
3.0.8
) should be used instead of (
3.0.7
) and that all the computations have to be meant in the limit
T\to+\infty
.

The positivity of
\mu_{t}
is preserved in time since Lemma
3.1
ensures that
\mu_{0}\geq 0
a.e. implies
\rho(x,\theta,t,h)=\mu_{t}(x,\theta,0,h)\geq 0
a.e. and therefore thanks to (
1.1.9
) we have also
\mu_{t}\geq 0
a.e..

Moreover, if
\mu_{0}
does not depend on
x
or on
(x,\theta)
neither
\rho(x,\theta,t,h)=\mu_{t}(x,\theta,0,h)
does thanks to Lemma
3.1
. Therefore, thanks to the representation formula (
1.1.9
), the same holds for
\mu_{t}
.

Now we only have to prove (
3.0.4
). To this purpose, if we use the representation (
1.1.9
), with triangle inequality and by extending the
L^{p}
norm of the second summand to all
s\in[0,+\infty)
, we get

(3.0.24)

\displaystyle\|\mu_{t}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot+t,\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}

(3.0.25)

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\mu_{t^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot,0,h^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}.

Now since the first term in (
3.0.24
) can be expressed as

\eqref{normaLpstep1}=\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot+t,\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}=\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[t,T+t]\times[-1,1])},

we focus on the second summand. Since

\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}=\left\|\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])},

using again Minkowski’s inequality, we can write

(
3.0.25
)

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\mu_{t^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot,0,h^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\|\mu_{t^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot,0,h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])},

and using (
3.0.9
) of Lemma
3.1
and applying triangle inequality to the external norm
\|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}
, we have

(
3.0.25
)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\left[\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\cdot,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}\right.\right.

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\cdot+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}

\displaystyle+C\left.\left.\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}\right]\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}.

By triangle inequality we get

(
3.0.25
)

(3.0.26)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\cdot,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}\right\|_{L^{p}}

(3.0.27)

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}\right\|_{L^{p}}

(3.0.28)

\displaystyle+C\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\cdot,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}\right\|_{L^{p}},

where in the previous inequality we shortened

L^{p}:=L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]),

and

\theta^{\prime}:=\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}).

Hereafter, we will assume that
p
is finite, but the case
p=\infty
can be studied in the same way.

By using Jensen’s inequality in the inner integral as in (
4.2.8
) and changing the integration order, one has

(
3.0.26
)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}^{p}\underbrace{(E(s,h)-E(s+t,h))^{p-1}}_{\leq 1}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}^{p}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})}_{=E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\leq 1}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,t]\times[-1,1])},

and, first applying again twice Jensen’s inequality as in (
4.2.8
), and then changing the integration order, one has also

\displaystyle\eqref{normaLpstep5}^{p}\leq\int d\theta dsdh\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}^{p}\xi,

with

\xi=\xi(t,s,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\underbrace{(1-E(t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))^{p-1}}_{\leq 1}\underbrace{(E(s,h)-E(s+t,h))^{p-1}}_{\leq 1})^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq 1,

and

\int d\theta dsdh:=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh.

Therefore

\displaystyle\eqref{normaLpstep5}\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}^{p}\int_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\xi_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

with

\xi_{1}=\xi_{1}(t,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})=E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\leq 1.

Hence

(
3.0.27
)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}^{p}\underbrace{\int_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})}_{=E(t-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\leq 1}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,t]\times\mathbb{[}-1,1])}.

Finally, one has

(
3.0.28
)

\displaystyle=C\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}}_{\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})}\left\|\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})}_{=E-E(\cdot+t,\cdot)\leq E}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)^{\frac{1}{p}}C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{X})},

where, in the first two lines of the previous inequality, we shortened

L^{p}:=L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]).

Summing (
3.0.24
) and (
3.0.25
), which is smaller than (
3.0.26
)+(
3.0.27
)+(
3.0.28
), we get (
3.0.4
).

Finally, (
3.0.5
) is obtained by sending
T\to+\infty
in (
3.0.4
).
∎

3.1.
Stationary solutions.

Now we briefly focus on the stationary solutions of the equations (
1.1.2
), (
1.1.2
) and (
1.1.2
). It is straightforward to see that the only stationary solutions (in
L^{1}
) of the three equations are:

•

\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)\equiv 0
for
x\in\mathbb{X}
, equation (
1.1.2
);

•

\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)\equiv\frac{c}{2\pi}E(s,h)
for
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
, equation (
1.1.2
);

•

\mu_{t}(\theta,s,h)\equiv\frac{c}{2\pi}E(s,h)
, equation (
1.1.2
);

•

\mu_{t}(s,h)\equiv cE(s,h)
, equation (
1.1.2
).

It is immediately checked that the previous mild solutions are stationary, because they correspond to
\mu_{t}(x,\theta,0,h)=c
, or
\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)=c
or
\mu_{t}(0,h)=c
, in the case of not dependance on
x
or
(x,\theta)
. Following
[
7
]
, the reverse implication can also be proved: these solutions are the only stationary ones. An alternative way to prove it is exactly to use Theorems
1.1
,
1.3
and Theorem
1.4
, since if a stationary solution converges to another solution, either strongly or weakly, then it coincides with it.

4.
The long time evolution of a density depending on
(\theta,s,h)
.

The aim of this Section is to prove Theorem
1.1
, which states that if
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}\cap L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
, with
p\in[1,+\infty]
, and
\mu_{t}
is a solution of

(4.0.1)

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(\theta,s,h)=\mu_{0}(\theta,s+t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{t^{\prime}}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),0,h^{\prime}),

then

\displaystyle\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\frac{\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}}}{t+1}+\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[t/4,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[t/4,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\right],

up for a constant
C>0
depending only on
Q
.

As we said in the introduction, the first term of (
4.0.1
) represents the probability density of the particles for which no collision has occurred until time
t
. The second one can be understood as the probability density that at least a collision has occurred before time
t
. From equation (
4.0.1
), we can preliminary notice that the long time behavior of
\mu_{t}
is determined by that of
\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)
, which is in turn determined by the equation

(4.0.2)

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)=\mu_{0}(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{t^{\prime}}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),0,h^{\prime}).

Now if
\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)=c
, by (
4.0.2
) we get
\mu_{0}(\theta,t,h)=cE(t,h)
and therefore by (
1.1.12
)
\mu_{t}\equiv cE
at any time
t
. Therefore the rate of convergence of
\mu_{t}(\theta,s,h)
to the equilibrium state is determined by the rate of convergence of
\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)
to the constant
\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}
. This is our purpose in the next steps.

4.1.
Writing
\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)
as a linear function of
\mu_{0}
.

We collect here some results that allow us to prove Theorem
1.1
. In particular we want to write
\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)
as a linear function of
\mu_{0}
to get a better estimate of its long time behavior. Therefore the first purpose is to prove now the following intermediate result.

Proposition 4.1
.

There exists a function
\varphi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]^{2};\mathbb{R})
depending only on the kernel
Q
such that for every
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
the function
(\theta,h)\mapsto\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)
writes as an affine function of
\varphi
and a linear function of
\mu_{0}
as

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(4.1.1)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\varphi(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

and moreover there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|\leq\frac{C}{t+1}\quad\forall(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]^{2}.

This Proposition is all what we need to prove Theorem
1.1
. We observe that the most similar to
\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}
is the third term in the right-hand side of (
4.1.1
). We will prove that the other terms vanish in the long time limit.

We are finally splitting the probability density
\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)
of a collision occurring at time
t
in four contributions. The first and the second one are understood respectively as the probability that the first and the second collision occur exactly at time
t
. The sum of third and the fourth one as the probability that at least two collisions happened before time
t
.

To prove Proposition
4.1
we make several steps by some preliminary Lemmas. We first recall the Definition
1.8
of the function
f
, that is,

\displaystyle f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime}):=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\partial h^{\prime\prime}(\theta+2\ell\pi,h^{\prime})}{\partial\theta}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime}(\theta+2\ell\pi,h^{\prime}))Q(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}(\theta+2\ell\pi,h^{\prime})|h^{\prime}),

with
h^{\prime\prime}
as in Definition
1.7

\displaystyle h^{\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime}):=\sin\left(\frac{\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})}{2}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{[2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-3\pi,2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-\pi)}(\theta).

Lemma
2.5
in the Section
2
establishes some properties of
f
: it has integral
1
and it is bounded by
\frac{C}{t}
.

We shall start the proof of Proposition
4.1
by proving the following Lemma.

Lemma 4.1
.

There exists a unique function
\varphi\in L^{\infty}_{loc}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]^{2})
such that

\displaystyle\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\pi}E(t,h)

(4.1.2)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})\varphi(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime}),

and moreover, for every
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
,
\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)
is an affine function of
\varphi
and a linear function of
\mu_{0}
:

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(4.1.3)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\varphi(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

We recall that
\varphi
is exactly the one in Proposition
4.1
. Therefore, once we will have proved it, we will only need to prove that, for large
t
,
\varphi
behaves as
\frac{1}{t}
at most.

We prove now Lemma
4.1

Proof.

We prove it in two steps.

Step 1:
we start by looking for a function
\gamma:\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]^{2}\to[0,+\infty)
such that for any
\mu_{0}
, the function
(\theta,h)\mapsto\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)
satisfies, for any
t>0,\theta,h

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(4.1.4)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\gamma(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

We want to separate the dependance on the initial datum
\mu_{0}
, and the last term, that is the convolution of
\gamma
and
\mu_{0}
, represents the probability that at least two collisions have occurred before time
t
.

To find such a function
\gamma
we write
\mu_{t}
as in the equation (
4.1.4
) and we substitute it in both sides of the equation (
4.0.2
). By doing this we get

(4.1.5)

\displaystyle\mu_{0}(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(4.1.6)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\gamma(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(4.1.7)

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(4.1.8)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(4.1.9)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\gamma(\tilde{\theta}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}),

with

\theta^{\prime\prime}=\theta^{\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}):=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime})\textrm{ and }\tilde{\theta}=\tilde{\theta}(\theta,h^{\prime}):=\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}).

The first two summands in (
4.1.5
) and (
4.1.7
) cancel the one each other, therefore we just have to write better the equality

\eqref{esprA}=\eqref{esprB}+\eqref{esprC}.

(
4.1.6
) is fine and does not need to be expressed in another way.

Instead we write better (
4.1.8
): first we change the integration order, and then we rename the variables (exchange
t^{\prime\prime}
with
t^{\prime}
and
h^{\prime}
with
h^{\prime\prime}
). By doing this we get

\displaystyle\eqref{esprB}=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\mu_{0}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime},\theta^{\prime\prime})\int_{0}^{t-t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})Q(t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime}),

and now we change variables from
h^{\prime\prime}
to
\theta^{\prime}
in such a way to get

\displaystyle\theta^{\prime\prime}=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime})=\theta^{\prime},

that is

\displaystyle h^{\prime\prime}=\sin\left(\frac{\theta-\theta^{\prime}+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})}{2}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{2\arcsin(h^{\prime})+[-3\pi,-\pi)}(\theta-\theta^{\prime})=h^{\prime\prime}(\theta-\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

as in Definition
1.7
.

Therefore, shortening
h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime\prime}(\theta-\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, we have obtained

(
4.1.8
)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})}^{\theta+3\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})}d\theta^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\frac{\partial h^{\prime\prime}}{\partial\theta}\int_{0}^{t-t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})Q(t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})}^{\theta+3\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})}d\theta^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\textrm{ by Definition \ref{defeffe} of }f

(4.1.10)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime}),

since the integrand is periodic with respect to
\theta^{\prime}
and therefore we can choose any period to compute the integral.

As for (
4.1.9
) we operate similarly to (
4.1.8
): first we change the integration order and we integrate before with respect to
(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
and then with respect to
(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, and then we exchange the variables names, that is, we exchange
t^{\prime\prime}
with
t^{\prime}
and
h^{\prime\prime}
with
h^{\prime}
. In this way we get

(4.1.11)

\displaystyle\eqref{esprC}=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t-t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})\gamma(t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime},\tilde{\theta}-\theta^{\prime}|h^{\prime}).

Therefore we obtained

\displaystyle\eqref{esprA}=\eqref{esprB}+\eqref{esprC}=\eqref{nuovaB}+\eqref{nuovaC},

which, for any
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}
, writes as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\gamma(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\biggl[f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t-t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})\gamma(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime})-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\biggr].

Therefore property (
4.1.4
) holds for any
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}
if and only if, for any
(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]^{2}
,
\gamma
verifies

(4.1.12)

\displaystyle\gamma(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})\gamma(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime}).

Now we show that, for fixed
h^{\prime}
,
\gamma(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot|h^{\prime})
exists in
\cap_{T>0}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])
and is non negative. To prove it, it is sufficient to use Lemma
3.1
, with
h^{\prime}
fixed, in the case: no dependance on
x
, with
p=\infty
and
\mu(\theta,t,h)=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})
.

We need an estimate in the space
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])
uniformly with respect to
h^{\prime}
with a possible dependance on
T
, that is an estimate in the space
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1]^{2})
. To get it, we recall that
\gamma
is obtained by using Lemma
3.1
, which is based on the Banach fixed-point Theorem, hence
\|\gamma(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot|h^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}
is bounded by a linear function of
\|f(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot|h^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}
. By (
2.3.7
) of Lemma
2.5
,
\|f(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot|h^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}
is uniformly bounded with respect to
h^{\prime}
, hence
\|\gamma(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot|h^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}
is too.

Step 2:
we want to use
\gamma-\frac{1}{2\pi}
instead of
\gamma
: since we would like to state that
\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}
, what we expect from
\gamma
by looking the equation (
4.1.4
) is that
\gamma(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}\frac{1}{2\pi}
. We intentionally do not precise the setting which this convergence is to be understood in, we will clarify it later.

Let us then define

\varphi:=\gamma-\frac{1}{2\pi}.

With this notation, by (
4.1.4
),
(\theta,h)\mapsto\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)
as a function of
\varphi
verifies:

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(\theta,0,h)

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}(\theta,t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{2\pi}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\varphi(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

and, using (
4.1.12
),
\varphi
solves

\displaystyle\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\pi}E(t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})\varphi(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime}),

with
f
of Definition
1.8
.

The equations above are exactly the properties (
4.1.2
) and (
4.1.3
).
∎

Now, as we said, the next step would be to deduce the long time behavior of
\varphi
from its definition in (
4.1.2
). But there are two problems in using directly (
4.1.2
). The first one is that the convolution kernel in the integral is
Q
, and the integral of
Q
is 1. If it was
<1
, one could have used Banach fixed-point Theorem to get long time estimates on
\varphi
. The second one is that the integral in (
4.1.2
) involves only the variables
(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, and the integral of
f-\frac{1}{2\pi}E
only with respect to
(t,h)
is non zero. Instead, by (
2.3.5
) of Lemma
2.5
, the integral of
f-\frac{1}{2\pi}E
with respect to
(\theta,t,h)
is zero. This suggests to iterate over (
4.1.2
) twice, so that, changing variables, we integrate also with respect to
\theta
.

Therefore, in the next two Lemmas, we get a more useful writing for
\varphi
and a property of
f-\frac{1}{2\pi}E
. To show their next use, we state both of them together.

We recall that
Q^{(n)}
and
E^{(n)}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
are defined in Definitions
1.5
and
1.6
.

Lemma 4.2
.

For any
c\in\mathbb{R}
the function
\varphi
defined in (
4.1.2
) of Lemma
4.1
verifies:

\displaystyle\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})f(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t,h)+\frac{c}{2\pi}\left[\int_{t}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}E^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})-E^{(2)}(t,h^{\prime})-E^{(3)}(t,h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\left[f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right].

Lemma 4.3
.

There exists a constant
\bar{c}>0
such that for any
c\in(0,\bar{c}]
it holds:

\displaystyle d:=\sup_{h\in[-1,1]}\left\|f(\cdot,\cdot,h|\cdot)-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}<1.

Before proving both lemmas above, we first show how they lead to the following estimate of
\varphi
that will be useful for the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Lemma 4.4
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that the function
\varphi
defined by (
4.1.2
) of Lemma
4.1
verifies

|\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|\leq\frac{C}{t+1}\quad\forall(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]^{2}.

Proof.

Fix
c\in(0,\bar{c}]
, with
\bar{c}
provided by Lemma
4.3
, and rewrite
\varphi
as in Lemma
4.2
with such
c
. Also denote

(4.1.13)

\displaystyle J(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})f(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})

(4.1.14)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t,h)+\frac{c}{2\pi}\left[\int_{t}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}E^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})-E^{(2)}(t,h^{\prime})-E^{(3)}(t,h^{\prime})\right],

and we use this notation only in this proof. With
J
the expression in Lemma
4.2
has the equivalent formulation:

\displaystyle\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=J(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})

(4.1.15)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\left[f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right].

We estimate the summands in (
4.1.13
) with Lemma
2.5
. The summands in (
4.1.14
) can be bounded by Lemma
2.3
. In this way we can state that the following quantities concerning
J
verify:

\displaystyle\|J\|_{L^{\infty}}<+\infty\quad\textrm{ and }\quad A_{J}:=\sup_{(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]^{2}}|tJ(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|<+\infty.

Step 1:
to begin with, we prove that
\varphi
is bounded. Denote

B_{T}^{\varphi}:=\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1]^{2})},

which is finite for any
T>0
thanks to Lemma
4.1
. If we now look at equation (
4.1.15
) we can write for
t\leq T

\displaystyle|\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{|J(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|}_{\leq\|J\|_{L^{\infty}}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\underbrace{|\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})|}_{\leq B_{t^{\prime}}^{\varphi}\leq B_{T}^{\varphi}}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|J\|_{L^{\infty}}+B_{T}^{\varphi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|J\|_{L^{\infty}}+B_{T}^{\varphi}\underbrace{\left\|f(\cdot,\cdot,h|\cdot)-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}}_{\leq d<1\enskip\forall h\textrm{ by Lemma }\ref{lemmaok}}

\displaystyle\leq\|J\|_{L^{\infty}}+B_{T}^{\varphi}d,

and therefore since the previous inequality holds for any
t\leq T
, the same holds for
B_{T}^{\varphi}=\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1]^{2})}
, that is

\displaystyle B_{T}^{\varphi}\leq\|J\|_{L^{\infty}}+B_{T}^{\varphi}d\quad\textrm{ and therefore}\quad B_{T}^{\varphi}\leq\frac{\|J\|_{L^{\infty}}}{1-d},

and since this inequality holds in turn regardless of
T
, this proves that

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1]^{2})}\leq\frac{\|J\|_{L^{\infty}}}{1-d}\quad\forall T>0\quad\textrm{ and therefore }\quad\varphi\in L^{\infty}.

Step 2:
we prove now that
\varphi
decays at most as
\frac{1}{t}
. Denote again

C_{T}^{\varphi}:=\sup_{(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1]^{2}}|t\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|,

which is again finite for fixed
T
thanks to Lemma
4.1
. Also fix
1<\alpha<\frac{1}{d}
, with
d<1
defined in Lemma
4.3
. If we use again the expression (
4.1.15
), for
t\leq T
we get

\displaystyle|t\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{|tJ(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|}_{\leq A_{J}}

\displaystyle+t\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{\alpha}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\underbrace{|\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})|}_{\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right|

\displaystyle+t\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{\frac{t}{\alpha}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\underbrace{|\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})|}_{\leq\frac{C_{t^{\prime}}^{\varphi}}{t^{\prime}}\leq\frac{\alpha C_{T}^{\varphi}}{t}}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq A_{J}+\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}t\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{\alpha}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right|

\displaystyle+\alpha C_{T}^{\varphi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{\frac{t}{\alpha}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\left|f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq A_{J}+\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}t\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{t(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\left|f(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right|

\displaystyle+\alpha C_{T}^{\varphi}\underbrace{\left\|f(\cdot,\cdot,h|\cdot)-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}}_{\leq d<1\hskip 3.5pt\forall h\textrm{ by Lemma }\ref{lemmaok}}

\displaystyle\leq A_{J}+\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}t\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{t(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\left[f(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})+\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right]+\alpha dC_{T}^{\varphi}.

We study here the second one of these three summands. The first term can be estimated as:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{t(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})}^{\infty}t^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}f(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{t(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=E^{(2)}\left(\frac{t(\alpha-1)}{\alpha},h\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha c_{2}}{t(\alpha-1)}\textrm{ by }\eqref{ubEn}\textrm{ of Lemma \ref{proprEn}}.

The second part can be estimated as:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{t(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=c\int_{t(1-\frac{1}{\alpha})}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}E^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha cc_{2}^{\prime}}{t(\alpha-1)}\textrm{ by }\eqref{restoEn}\textrm{ of Lemma \ref{proprEn}}.

Finally we got

\displaystyle|t\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|\leq A_{J}+\frac{\alpha\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}(c_{2}+cc_{2}^{\prime})}{\alpha-1}+\alpha dC_{T}^{\varphi}\quad\forall t\leq T.

Passing the inequality above to the supremum on all variables, we obtain:

C_{T}^{\varphi}\leq A_{J}+\frac{\alpha\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}(c_{2}+cc_{2}^{\prime})}{\alpha-1}+\alpha dC_{T}^{\varphi},\hskip 8.50012pt\textrm{ therefore }\hskip 8.50012ptC_{T}^{\varphi}\leq\frac{A_{J}+\frac{\alpha\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}(c_{2}+cc_{2}^{\prime})}{\alpha-1}}{1-\alpha d}\hskip 8.50012pt\forall T>0,

i.e.

\sup_{(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]^{2}}|t\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|\leq\frac{A_{J}+\frac{\alpha\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}(c_{2}+cc_{2}^{\prime})}{\alpha-1}}{1-\alpha d},

which concludes the proof.
∎

We show now how both Lemmas
4.2
and
4.3
imply Proposition
4.1
.

4.1.1.
Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Proof.

The function
\varphi
in the statement of Proposition
4.1
is exactly the function
\varphi
defined by Lemma
4.1
. By Lemma
4.4
, such function
\varphi
satisfies:

|\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})|\leq\frac{C}{t+1}\quad\forall(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]^{2},

that is, the estimate in the statement of Proposition
4.1
.
∎

We now prove Lemmas
4.2
and
4.3
.

4.1.2.
Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Proof.

We split up the proof into three steps.

Step 1:
First we prove here that the function
\varphi
defined through equation (
4.1.2
) of Lemma
4.1
satisfies the following equality:

\displaystyle\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})f(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})

(4.1.16)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime}),

with
E^{(2)}
from Definition
1.6
.

We begin by noticing that we can iterate twice the map defining
\varphi
in Lemma
4.1
. This can be done substituting the whole right-hand side of (
4.1.2
) into the
\varphi
inside the integral. In this way we get

(4.1.17)

\displaystyle\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})

(4.1.18)

\displaystyle=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})f(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})

(4.1.19)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi}E(t,h)-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})E(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(4.1.20)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime\prime\prime})\varphi(\tilde{\theta},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}),

with

\tilde{\theta}=\tilde{\theta}(\theta,h^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}):=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime\prime}).

Now the term in (
4.1.17
) is fine, but the one in (
4.1.19
) can be written better than that by using
E^{(2)}
of Definition
1.6
. Indeed property (
2.2.3
) of Lemma
2.3
implies that

\displaystyle\eqref{termE2}=-\frac{1}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t,h).

As for the term in (
4.1.20
), by Fubini-Tonelli Theorem, we can change again the integration order: before we integrate with respect to
(t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
and then with respect to
t^{\prime}
. Then we exchange the names of the variables, this time only
t^{\prime\prime}
with
t^{\prime}
. In this way, we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{termphi}=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\varphi(\tilde{\theta},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t-t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})Q(t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime\prime\prime}).

After a change of variables

\tilde{\theta}=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime})=\theta^{\prime},

in the right-hand side, we obtain

(
4.1.20
)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\int_{\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime\prime})}^{\theta+3\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime\prime})}d\theta^{\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})\textrm{ with }f\textrm{ of Definition \ref{defeffe}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime}),

because the integrand is again periodic in
\theta^{\prime}
and therefore any interval of length
2\pi
is fine.

Summing (
4.1.18
) with reformulated (
4.1.19
) and (
4.1.20
), we get property (
4.1.16
).

Step 2:
We prove now that

(4.1.21)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\varphi(\theta,t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h)=\int_{t}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dhE^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h)-E^{(2)}(t,h^{\prime})-E^{(3)}(t,h^{\prime}).

To prove property (
4.1.21
), we compute the integral
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh
of both sides of equation (
4.1.16
). We focus on the summands in the right-hand side of the identity (
4.1.16
), since the integral of the left-hand side does not need to be changed.

The first one is immediate:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dhf(\theta,s,h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta f(\theta,t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\textrm{ by changing integration order}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\textrm{ by property \eqref{intfQ2}}

\displaystyle=1-E^{(2)}(t,h^{\prime})\textrm{ by Definition }\ref{def:En},

and the second one is very similar

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})f(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta f(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})}_{=Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\textrm{ by property \eqref{intfQ2}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})}_{=Q^{(3)}(t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\textrm{ by Definition }\ref{def:Qn}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ^{(3)}(t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})=1-E^{(3)}(t,h^{\prime})\textrm{ by Definition }\ref{def:En}.

Notice that by property (
2.2.6
) of Lemma
2.3
,
E^{(2)}
has integral
2
with respect to variables
(s,h)
, so the third term of the sum above writes as:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\frac{1}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h)=2-\int_{t}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dhE^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h).

The integral of the fourth summand instead can be written as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})}_{=Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})\textrm{ by property \eqref{intfQ2}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\int_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ^{(2)}(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\left(1-\int_{t-t^{\prime\prime}}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right)\textrm{ by \eqref{Qnint1} of Lemma \ref{proprQn}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})(1-E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}))\textrm{ by Definition \ref{def:En}}.

Summing upon all the integral of the terms in the right-hand side of equation (
4.1.16
) we get equation (
4.1.21
).

Step 3:
we can conclude. If we subtract and add back to both sides of (
4.1.16
) the quantity

\displaystyle\frac{c}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}),

where
c\in\mathbb{R}
, we get

\displaystyle\varphi(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})f(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})f(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\left[f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime\prime})-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{c}{2\pi}\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})E^{(2)}(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})}_{=\eqref{passo2}},

and this concludes the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Now prove Lemma
4.3
.

4.1.3.
Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

Proof.

We denote here
f^{h}(\theta,t,h^{\prime})=f(\theta,t,h|h^{\prime})
, and we also write
\int d\theta dtdh^{\prime}:=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}
. So we get

\displaystyle\left\|f^{h}-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=\int_{f^{h}>\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}f^{h}-\frac{c}{2\pi}\int_{f^{h}>\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}

\displaystyle-\int_{f^{h}\leq\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}f^{h}+\frac{c}{2\pi}\int_{f^{h}\leq\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int d\theta dtdh^{\prime}f^{h}}_{=1\textrm{ by }\eqref{fint1dinuovo}\textrm{ of Lemma \ref{propreffe}}}-2\underbrace{\int_{f^{h}\leq\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}f^{h}}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle-c\left[\frac{2}{2\pi}\int_{f^{h}>\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}-\underbrace{\frac{1}{2\pi}\int d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}}_{=2\textrm{ by \eqref{intEn} of Lemma \ref{proprEn}}}\right]
