\displaystyle\leq 1-2c\left[\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{f^{h}>\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}-1\right]

\displaystyle=1-2c\left[\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{f^{h}>0}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}-1\right]+2c\int_{0<f^{h}\leq\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}.

Now we first prove that the second of these three summands divided by
2c
is uniformly negative for
h\in[-1,1]
(next
Step 1
), and then we prove that the third one divided by
c
is infinitesimal as
c\to 0
(next
Step 2
). Once done this, we could conclude, indeed we would have

(4.1.22)

\displaystyle\left\|f^{h}-\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 1-2c\underbrace{\left[\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{f^{h}>0}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}-1\right]}_{\geq C>0\enskip\forall h\in[-1,1]\textrm{ by using }\textbf{Step 1}}+2c\underbrace{\int d\theta dtdh^{\prime}_{0<f^{h}\leq\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}E^{(2)}}_{\leq\frac{C}{2}\textrm{ for }c\leq\bar{c}<<1\enskip\forall h\in[-1,1]\textrm{ by }\textbf{Step 2}}

(4.1.23)

\displaystyle\leq 1-cC\textrm{ for }c<<1.

Therefore we begin with the proof of
Step 1
: we have to prove that the first summand in (
4.1.22
) is uniformly positive as
c\to 0
.

Step 1:
we prove here that

(4.1.24)

\displaystyle\inf_{h\in[-1,1]}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{f^{h}>0}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}-1>0.

The easier way to prove this is by finding a domain

\Omega\subseteq\left\{(\theta,t,h^{\prime}):t\geq 0,h\in[-1,1],\theta\in[2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-3\pi,2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-\pi]\right\},

where each
f^{h}
is positive. We are abusing a little bit the notation, since we are treating
f^{h}
as if it was defined for
\theta\in[2\arcsin(h)-3\pi,2\arcsin(h)-\pi]
. To find such a domain, we notice that

(4.1.25)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial h^{\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime})}{\partial\theta}>0\quad\forall\theta\in(2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-3\pi,2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-\pi),h^{\prime}\in[-1,1],

that is, the Jacobian determinant does not affect the region where
f^{h}
is positive.

We also observe that for
t\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
we have

(4.1.26)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\underbrace{Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})}_{=\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}\textrm{ since }t-t^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{2}}\underbrace{Q(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})}_{=\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}\textrm{ since }t^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{36}{\pi^{4}}t>0,

and for
t\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
we have

(4.1.27)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})Q(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})\geq\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}dt^{\prime}\underbrace{Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})}_{=\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}\textrm{ since }t-t^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{2}}\underbrace{Q(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})}_{=\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}\textrm{ since }t^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{36}{\pi^{4}}(1-t)>0.

Combining the properties (
4.1.25
), (
4.1.26
) and (
4.1.27
), we get

f^{h}(\theta,t,h^{\prime})>0\quad\forall t\in(0,1),\theta\in(2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-3\pi,2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-\pi),h,h^{\prime}\in[-1,1].

Therefore, if we define

\Omega:=\left\{(\theta,t,h^{\prime}):t\in(0,1),h^{\prime}\in[-1,1],\theta\in(2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-3\pi,2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-\pi)\right\},

we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{f^{h}>0}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{1}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-3\pi}^{2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-\pi}d\theta E^{(2)}(t,h^{\prime})

(4.1.28)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}E^{(2)}(t,h^{\prime}).

Now we have to bound from below the quantity (
4.1.28
). To this end, we observe that since
Q\leq\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}
, then

(4.1.29)

\displaystyle Q^{(2)}(t,h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime\prime})Q(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\leq\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\frac{36}{\pi^{4}}=\frac{72}{\pi^{4}}t,

and therefore, as for the right-hand side of (
4.1.28
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}E^{(2)}(t,h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dt\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{t}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\min\{1,t^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})t^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{1}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})}_{=2-\int_{0}^{1}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\textrm{ by property }\eqref{Qnint1}}

\displaystyle=2-\int_{0}^{1}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\underbrace{Q^{(2)}(t^{\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})}_{\leq\frac{72}{\pi^{4}}t^{\prime}\textrm{ thanks to }\eqref{ub}}(1-t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq 2-\int_{0}^{1}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\frac{72}{\pi^{4}}t^{\prime}(1-t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=2-\frac{288}{\pi^{4}}\frac{1}{6}=2-\frac{48}{\pi^{4}}>1,

and this concludes the proof of property (
4.1.24
), i.e.,
Step 1
.

Now we can prove
Step 2
, that is, the second summand in (
4.1.22
) divided by
c
is infinitesimal as
c\to 0
.

Step 2:
we want to prove that

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in[-1,1]}\int_{0<f^{h}\leq\frac{c}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}d\theta dtdh^{\prime}E^{(2)}\xrightarrow[c\to 0]{}0.

Arguing by contradiction, there would exist
\varepsilon>0
, a sequence
c_{n}\in[0,\frac{1}{n}]
and another sequence
h^{n}\in[-1,1]
such that

\displaystyle\varepsilon\leq\int_{0<f^{h^{n}}\leq\frac{c_{n}}{2\pi}E^{(2)}}E^{(2)}.

By compactness, there would exist a subsequence
{h}^{n_{k}}
of
\{h^{n}\}
converging to
\bar{h}\in[-1,1]
such that

E^{(2)}\mathbbm{1}_{0<f^{h^{n_{k}}}\leq\frac{c_{n_{k}}}{2\pi}}\xrightarrow[k\to+\infty]{}E^{(2)}\mathbbm{1}_{0<f^{\bar{h}}\leq 0}=0\textrm{ everywhere in }(\theta,t,h^{\prime}),

moreover
E^{(2)}\mathbbm{1}_{0<f^{h^{n_{k}}}\leq\frac{c_{n_{k}}}{2\pi}}\leq E^{(2)}
, and therefore this can not occur for dominated convergence of
\{E^{(2)}\mathbbm{1}_{0<f^{h^{n_{k}}}\leq\frac{c_{n_{k}}}{2\pi}}\}_{k}
.

By (
4.1.23
), that concludes the proof.
∎

4.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Now we have all the intermediate results that we need to prove Theorem
1.1
.

Proof.

The first result we prove is the statement (
1.2.2
) of the Theorem. That is, we want to prove that for any function
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}\cap L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])

\displaystyle\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}}{t+1}

\displaystyle+C\left[\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[t/4,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[t/4,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\right].

We prove this only for
p\in[1,+\infty)
, since the proof of the statement in the case
p=\infty
follows in the same way as the proof for finite
p
. When not better specified, in this proof we will shorten

\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}}:=\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])},

and we will often use also the notation

\int d\theta dsdh:=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh.

To begin with, we recall Proposition
4.1
, and inserting it into (
1.1.12
) we get

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(\theta,s,h)

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}(\theta,s+t,h)+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta_{1},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(4.2.1)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta_{2}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}),

with

\displaystyle\theta_{1}

\displaystyle=\theta_{1}(\theta,h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}):=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle\theta_{2}

\displaystyle=\theta_{2}(\theta,h^{\prime}):=\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}).

Since the fourth summand in (
4.2.1
) writes as

(
4.2.1
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\underbrace{\int_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})}_{=E(s,h)-E(s+t-t^{\prime\prime},h)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})[E(s,h)-E(s+t-t^{\prime},h)],

we have

\displaystyle\mu_{t}(\theta,s,h)

(4.2.2)

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}(\theta,s+t,h)

(4.2.3)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(4.2.4)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta_{1},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(4.2.5)

\displaystyle+\frac{E(s,h)}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(4.2.6)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}E(s+t-t^{\prime},h)\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(4.2.7)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\varphi(\theta_{2}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}).

To prove inequality (
1.2.2
) in the first part of the Theorem we have to estimate all the terms in the expression above.

As for the first one we have

\displaystyle\|\eqref{addendo1}\|_{L^{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{0}(\theta,s+t,h)|^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{t}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{0}(\theta,s,h)|^{p}=\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[t,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p},

and therefore the estimate in (
1.2.2
) applies to it.

A very simple but crucial inequality in the next estimates is the following: if
f\geq 0
, then by Jensen’s inequality applied when the measure is
dxf(x)
over the set
X
we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{X}dxf(x)g(x)\right|^{p}\leq\int_{X}dxf(x)|g(x)|^{p}\left(\int_{X}dxf(x)\right)^{p-1}.

Applying Jensen’s inequality with
f=Q
,
g=\mu_{0}
and
X=[t_{1},t_{2}]\times[-1,1]
, one gets

\displaystyle\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|^{p}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\right)^{p-1}

(4.2.8)

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|^{p}\left(E(t-t_{2},h)-E(t-t_{1},h)\right)^{p-1}.

Going back to the proof, the second term in the sum can be bounded as follows

\displaystyle\|\eqref{addendo2}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(4.2.9)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(4.2.10)

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

We shall estimate separately (
4.2.9
) and (
4.2.10
). As for the first one, thanks to Jensen’s inequality used as in (
4.2.8
), we have

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo2first}^{p}\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\xi_{0}

with

\displaystyle\xi_{0}

\displaystyle=\xi_{0}(s,t,h):=\left(E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)-E(s+t,h)\right)^{p-1}\leq E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2C)^{p-1}}{(t+2)^{p-1}}\textrm{ by Lemma \ref{decE}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo2first}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2C)^{p-1}}{(t+2)^{p-1}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\underbrace{E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{\leq\frac{C}{t-t^{\prime}+1}\leq\frac{2C}{t+2}\textrm{ by Lemma \ref{decE}}}|\mu_{0}(\theta,{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime})|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2C)^{p}}{(t+2)^{p}}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p},

while the second one satisfies

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo2second}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})|^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\underbrace{\left(E(s,h)-E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)\right)^{p-1}}_{\leq 1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\underbrace{E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{\leq 1}|\mu_{0}(\theta,{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime})|^{p}\leq\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{2},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p}.

which ends the estimate of (
4.2.3
) since

\|\eqref{addendo2}\|_{L^{p}}\leq\eqref{addendo2first}+\eqref{addendo2second}\leq\frac{2C}{t+2}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}}+\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{2},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}.

Then, as for (
4.2.4
), if we split
\{t^{\prime}\in[0,t]\}
in
\{t^{\prime}\in[0,\frac{t}{2}]\}\cup\{t^{\prime}\in[\frac{t}{2},t]\}
and
\{t^{\prime\prime}\in[0,t^{\prime}]\}
in
\{t^{\prime\prime}\in[0,\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}]\}\cup\{t^{\prime\prime}\in[\frac{t^{\prime}}{2},t^{\prime}]\}
, we get

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo3}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta_{1},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right\|_{L^{p}}

(4.2.11)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int d\theta dsdh\left|\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta_{1},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(4.2.12)

\displaystyle+\left(\int d\theta dsdh\left|\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta_{1},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(4.2.13)

\displaystyle+\left(\int d\theta dsdh\left|\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta_{1},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

with
\theta_{1}
as before. Now estimate the three summands separately.

Let us begin with the first one: using twice Jensen’s inequality (
4.2.8
), one gets

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo3first}^{p}\leq\int d\theta dsdh\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|^{p}\xi_{1},

with

\displaystyle\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=\xi_{1}(s,t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\left(E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)-E(s+t,h)\right)^{p-1}(1-E(t^{\prime},h^{\prime}))^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)^{p-1}\leq\frac{(2C)^{p-1}}{(t+2)^{p-1}}\textrm{ by Lemma \ref{decE}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo3first}^{p}\leq\frac{(2C)^{p-1}}{(t+2)^{p-1}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|^{p}\int_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})\xi_{1,2}

with

\displaystyle\xi_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\xi_{1,2}(t,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhQ(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})=E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\leq E(\frac{t}{2},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C}{t+2}\textrm{ by Lemma \ref{decE}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo3first}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2C)^{p}}{(t+2)^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|^{p}\underbrace{\int_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})}_{\leq 1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2C)^{p}}{(t+2)^{p}}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

As for (
4.2.12
), we use twice Jensen’s inequality (
4.2.8
) to get

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo3second}^{p}\leq\int d\theta dsdh\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{0}^{\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|^{p}\xi_{2},

with

\displaystyle\xi_{2}

\displaystyle=\xi_{2}(s,t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\left(E(s,h)-E\left(s+\frac{t}{2},h\right)\right)^{p-1}\left(E\left(\frac{t^{\prime}}{2},h^{\prime}\right)-E(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq E\left(\frac{t^{\prime}}{2},h^{\prime}\right)^{p-1}\leq\frac{(2C)^{p-1}}{(t^{\prime}+2)^{p-1}}\leq\frac{(4C)^{p-1}}{(t+4)^{p-1}}\textrm{ by Lemma \ref{decE}}.

Therefore, if

\bar{t}:=\max\left\{2t^{\prime\prime},\frac{t}{2}\right\},

we have

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo3second}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(4C)^{p-1}}{(t+4)^{p-1}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|^{p}\int_{\bar{t}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\underbrace{Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})}_{\leq\frac{2C}{t^{\prime}+2}\leq\frac{4C}{t+4}\textrm{ from \eqref{decQ}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(4C)^{p}}{(t+4)^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|^{p}\underbrace{\int_{\max\{2t^{\prime\prime},\frac{t}{2}\}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{\leq 1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(4C)^{p}}{(t+4)^{p}}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

For (
4.2.13
), applying twice Jensen’s inequality as in (
4.2.8
), we also have

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo3third}^{p}\leq\int d\theta dsdh\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\int_{\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|^{p}\xi_{3},

with

\displaystyle\xi_{3}=\xi_{3}(s,t,h,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\left(E(s,h)-E\left(s+\frac{t}{2},h\right)\right)^{p-1}\left(1-E\left(\frac{t^{\prime}}{2},h^{\prime}\right)\right)^{p-1}\leq 1.

Hence

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo3third}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{\frac{t}{4}}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta,t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|^{p}\underbrace{\int_{\max\{\frac{t}{2},t^{\prime\prime}\}}^{\min\{t,2t^{\prime\prime}\}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\underbrace{E(t-t^{\prime},h^{\prime})}_{\leq 1}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})}_{\leq 1}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p}.

Thus

\|\eqref{addendo3}\|_{L^{p}}\leq\eqref{addendo3first}+\eqref{addendo3second}+\eqref{addendo3third}\leq\left[\frac{2C}{t+2}+\frac{4C}{t+4}\right]\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}}+\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])},

and we have ended with the estimate of (
4.2.4
).

Now we shall bound (
4.2.5
), and, since
E\leq 1
implies
E^{p}\leq E
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\eqref{addendo4}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime\prime})-\langle\mu_{0}\rangle\right|^{p}E(s,h)^{p}

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime\prime})-\langle\mu_{0}\rangle\right|^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhE(s,h)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{t}^{\infty}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime\prime})|\right)^{p}=\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[t,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p}.

For the fifth summand one can write

\displaystyle\|\eqref{addendo5}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}E(s+t-t^{\prime},h)\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(4.2.14)

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)^{\frac{1}{p}-1}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}E(s+t-t^{\prime},h)\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(4.2.15)

\displaystyle+(2\pi)^{\frac{1}{p}-1}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}E(s+t-t^{\prime},h)\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

and we bound again (
4.2.14
) and (
4.2.15
) separately. This way we get

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo5first}^{p}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{1-p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\underbrace{E(s+t-t^{\prime},h)}_{\leq E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)^{1-p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\underbrace{E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)^{p}}_{\leq\frac{C^{p}}{(s+\frac{t}{2}+1)^{p}}\mathbbm{1}(s+\frac{t}{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-|h|})\textrm{ by Lemma \ref{decE}}}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\frac{1}{(s+\frac{t}{2}+1)^{p+1}}\leq\frac{(2C)^{p}}{p(t+2)^{p}}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p},

and, since
E(s,h)\leq 1
implies
E(s,h)^{p}\leq E(s,h)
, we also have

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo5second}^{p}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{1-p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\underbrace{E(s+t-t^{\prime},h)}_{\leq E(s,h)}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}|\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},{t^{\prime}},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)^{1-p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhE(s,h)^{p}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{2},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p}\leq\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{2},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p},

and therefore

\displaystyle\|\eqref{addendo5}\|_{L^{p}}\leq\eqref{addendo5first}+\eqref{addendo5second}\leq\frac{2C}{t+2}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+C\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{2},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}.

We can estimate the sixth term in the same way as we estimated (
4.2.4
): we split the integral with respect to
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}
in

[0,t]=\left[0,\frac{t}{2}\right]\cup\left[\frac{t}{2},t\right]\textrm{ and }[0,t^{\prime}]=\left[0,\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}\right]\cup\left[\frac{t^{\prime}}{2},t^{\prime}\right],

in such a way to have

\displaystyle\|\eqref{addendo6}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\varphi(\theta_{2}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})||\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right\|_{L^{p}}

(4.2.16)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\varphi(\theta_{2}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})||\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right\|_{L^{p}}

(4.2.17)

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\varphi(\theta_{2}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})||\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right\|_{L^{p}}

(4.2.18)

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(\cdot+t-t^{\prime},\cdot|h^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\varphi(\theta_{2}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})||\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right\|_{L^{p}},

and then we use again Jensen’s inequality (
4.2.8
).

We begin with (
4.2.16
). Arguing as we said, we get

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo6first}^{p}\leq\int d\theta dsdh\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\varphi||\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}\xi_{4},

with

|\varphi|=|\varphi(\theta_{2}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\leq C\textrm{ by Proposition \ref{prop:musvarphi}},

and

\displaystyle\xi_{4}

\displaystyle:=\xi_{4}(s,t,h)=\left(E\left(s+\frac{t}{2},h\right)-E(s+t,h)\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{p-1}}{(s+\frac{t}{2}+1)^{p-1}}\mathbbm{1}\left(s+\frac{t}{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-|h|}\right)\textrm{ by Lemma \ref{decE}}.

In this way, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo6first}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi C^{2p-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\frac{1}{(s+\frac{t}{2}+1)^{p-1}}\mathbbm{1}(s+\frac{t}{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-|h|})\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p}

\displaystyle=2\pi C^{2p-1}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\frac{1}{(s+\frac{t}{2}+1)^{p-1}}\int_{-1}^{1}dh\mathbbm{1}(1-|h|\leq\frac{1}{s+\frac{t}{2}})\underbrace{(E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)-E(s+t,h))}_{\leq E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)\leq\frac{C}{s+\frac{t}{2}+1}\textrm{ by Lemma \ref{decE}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi C^{2p}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p}\int_{\frac{t}{2}+1}^{\infty}ds\frac{1}{s^{p+1}}=2\pi\frac{2^{p}C^{2p}}{p(t+2)^{p}}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p}.

We still have to estimate the other two terms: the second one can be bounded using first (
4.2.8
) and then Proposition
4.1
, as follows. We have

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo6second}^{p}\leq\int d\theta dsdh\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\varphi||\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p}\xi_{5}

with

\xi_{5}=\xi_{5}(t,s,h):=\left(E(s,h)-E\left(s+\frac{t}{2},h\right)\right)^{p-1}\leq E(s,h)^{p-1}\leq 1.

Since

|\varphi|=|\varphi(\theta_{2}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\leq\frac{C}{t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime}+1}\leq\frac{2C}{t^{\prime}+2}\leq\frac{4C}{t+4}\textrm{ by Proposition \ref{prop:musvarphi}},

we have

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo6second}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(4C)^{p}}{(t+4)^{p}}2\pi\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\underbrace{\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})}_{=E(s,h)-E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)\leq E(s,h)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(4C)^{p}}{(t+4)^{p}}2\pi\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhE(s,h)=\frac{(4C)^{p}}{(t+4)^{p}}2\pi\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}^{p}.

Finally, only (
4.2.18
) is missing: we estimate it below.

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo6third}^{p}\leq\int d\theta dsdh\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\xi_{6}\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{\frac{t^{\prime}}{2}}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}|\varphi||\mu_{0}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})|\right|^{p},

with

\xi_{6}=\xi_{6}(t,s,h):=\left(E(s,h)-E(s+\frac{t}{2},h)\right)^{p-1}\leq 1,

and

|\varphi|=|\varphi(\theta_{2}-\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})|\leq C\textrm{ by Proposition \ref{prop:musvarphi}},

and therefore

\displaystyle\eqref{addendo6third}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi C^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t^{\prime}}{2},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi C^{p}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\underbrace{(E(s,h)-E(s+\frac{t}{2},h))}_{\leq E(s,h)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi C^{p}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{p}.

Thus we get

\displaystyle\|\eqref{addendo6}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\eqref{addendo6first}+\eqref{addendo6second}+\eqref{addendo6third}

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left[\left(\frac{2C^{2}}{t+2}+\frac{4C}{t+4}\right)\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+C\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\right],

which terminates the proof of the first statement (
1.2.2
) of Theorem
1.1
.

Now it remains to prove the second part of the Theorem, that is, (
1.2.3
), which states that in the particular case
\mu_{0}(\theta,s,h)=\mu_{in}(\theta)E(s,h)

\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\leq\frac{C}{t+1}\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})}.

To this purpose, notice that we can estimate the four summands in the right-hand side of (
1.2.2
) as follows. For any
q\geq 1
we have

\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[A,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}=\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})}\|E\|_{L^{q}([A,+\infty)\times[-1,1])},

and the terms in the right-hand side of (
1.2.2
) correspond to the four cases
q=1
and
A=0
,
q=1
and
A=\frac{t}{4}
,
q=p
and
A=0
,
q=p
and
A=\frac{t}{4}
. Moreover
\|E\|_{L^{\infty}([A,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\leq\frac{C}{A+1}
from Lemma
2.2
, while for finite
q
we have

\displaystyle\|E\|_{L^{q}([A,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{A}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhE(s,h)^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{A}^{\infty}ds\frac{1}{(s+1)^{q+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq\frac{C}{A+1},

and therefore:

•

if
q=1
and
A=0
then

\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[A,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\leq\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})}\leq\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})};

•

if
q=p
and
A=0
then

\displaystyle\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[A,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})}\underbrace{\|E\|_{L^{p}([0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}}_{\leq(\int dsdhE(s,h)^{p})^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq(\int dsdhE(s,h))^{\frac{1}{p}}=1}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})};

•

if
q=1
and
A=\frac{t}{4}
then

\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[A,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\leq\underbrace{\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})}}_{\leq\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})}}\frac{4C}{t+4}\leq\frac{4C}{t+4}\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})};

•

if
q=p
and
A=\frac{t}{4}
then

\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[A,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\leq\frac{4C}{t+4}\|\mu_{in}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi})}.

By substituting these estimates into (
1.2.2
) we get (
1.2.3
).
∎

5.
The long time evolution of a density depending on
(x,\theta,s,h)
.

In this Section we want to prove Theorems
1.2
,
1.3
and
1.4
, that we recall below for clarity. Let the Fourier coefficients of a mild solution of (
1.1.9
) with initial datum
\mu_{0}
be

\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,s,h):=\int dxe^{2\pi ik\cdot x}\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h),\qquad k\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\textrm{ or }\mathbb{Z}^{2},

where the integral over
x
is performed over
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
or
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
depending on whenever the solution is defined for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
or
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
and the same holds for the ambiguity
k\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
or
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Theorem
1.2
states there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only
Q
and not on
p
, such that for any
k\in\mathbb{R}^{2},k\neq(0,0)

\left\|\mu_{t}^{k}\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq\frac{C}{\min\{1,|k|^{6}\}}\left[\frac{\|\mu_{0}^{k}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\mu_{0}^{k}\|_{L^{p}}}{t+1}+\|\mu_{0}^{k}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\|\mu_{0}^{k}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\right].

When not specified, the
L^{1}
and
L^{p}
norms are taken where the coefficients are defined, that is
\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]
.

On the flat torus
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, this result combined with Theorem
1.1
implies Theorem
1.3
, that is, under suitable conditions on
\mu_{0}
, whenever
p
is finite

\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}0,

and the same result holds for the weak-
*
convergence in
L^{\infty}
.

On
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, Theorem
1.2
implies that
\mu_{t}\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}0
(Theorem
1.4
) in a very weak sense (of course not in
L^{1}
because the total mass is preserved).

5.1.
Long time behavior of Fourier coefficients.

The first goal is to prove Theorem
1.2
. To this purpose we make several steps. First we characterize the time evolution of a particular Fourier coefficient: by multiplying equation (
1.1.9
) times
e^{2\pi ik\cdot x}
and integrating with respect to
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
(or
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
) we get

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,s,h)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi itk\cdot v(\theta)}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta,s+t,h)

(5.1.1)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\mu_{t^{\prime}}^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),0,h^{\prime}),

and evaluating the obtained result at
s=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,0,h)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi itk\cdot v(\theta)}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta,t,h)

(5.1.2)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\mu_{t^{\prime}}^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),0,h^{\prime}).

As in the case without dependance on the variable
x
, the long time behavior of
\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,s,h)
is fully characterized by the long time behavior of
\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,s=0,h)
. That is the next step.

5.1.1.
Writing
\mu_{t}^{k}
as a linear function of
\mu_{0}^{k}
.

As in the case
k=(0,0)
we studied in Section
4
, we want to separate the dependance on
\mu_{0}^{k}
in order to get more precise estimates. In particular, what we need to prove Theorem
1.2
is the following result.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
(or
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
) and let
\{\mu_{t}^{k}\}
be the Fourier coefficients of the mild solution of (
1.1.9
) defined in (
1.2.4
). For any
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},k\neq(0,0)
(respectively
k\in\mathbb{R}^{2},k\neq(0,0)
) there exists a function
\varphi^{k}:\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[-1,1]\xrightarrow{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{C}
such that
\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,0,h)
writes as a linear function of
\mu_{0}^{k}
and an affine function of
\varphi^{k}
as

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,0,h)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi itk\cdot v(\theta)}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}))}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\varphi^{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta^{\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

and moreover there exists a constant
C
depending only on
Q
(and not on
k
) such that

|\varphi^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})|\leq\frac{C}{\min\{1,|k|^{6}\}(t+1)}\hskip 17.00024pt\forall(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[-1,1].

This Proposition allows us to prove Theorem
1.2
since none of the summands which
\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,0,h)
is composed of survives for large
t
. To prove this Proposition, we recall the Definition
1.7
of
h^{\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime})
, that is

h^{\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime}):=\sin\left(\frac{\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})}{2}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{2\arcsin(h^{\prime})+[-3\pi,-\pi)}(\theta),

and this time, instead of using
f(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, we use
g^{k}
as in Definition
1.9
, that is,

\displaystyle g^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\partial h^{\prime\prime}}{\partial\theta}e^{2\pi itk\cdot v(\theta)}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})Q(t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot[v(\theta^{\prime}-\pi+2\arcsin(h^{\prime}))-v(\theta)]},

with

h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime\prime}(\theta-\theta^{\prime}+2\ell\pi,h^{\prime}),\textrm{ and }h^{\prime\prime}\textrm{ from Definition \ref{defh''}}.

Of course, because of the complex exponential,
g^{k}
is no more a function of
\theta-\theta^{\prime}
, differently from
f
of Definition
1.8
in the previous Section. Nevertheless, it is still periodic both with respect to
\theta
and to
\theta^{\prime}
. In Lemma
2.6
in Section
2
we collect some properties of the functions
g^{k}
,
k\neq(0,0)
.

The first necessary step to prove Proposition
5.1
is the following Lemma, that proves the existence of the function
\varphi^{k}
, but not its decaying properties in long time, that we will study immediately after.

Lemma 5.1
.

For any
k\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
k\neq(0,0)
, there exists a unique function
\varphi^{k}\in L^{\infty}_{loc}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[-1,1])
satisfying

\displaystyle\varphi^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=g^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(5.1.3)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\varphi^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

and moreover for any
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
or
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
,
\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,0,h)
writes as a function of
\varphi^{k}
and
\mu_{0}
as:

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,0,h)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi itk\cdot v(\theta)}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}))}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(5.1.4)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\varphi^{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta^{\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

Proof.

The proof of this Lemma is very similar to the proof of Lemma
4.1
in the previous Section
4
, but we write it entirely because of the presence of the complex exponential that changes some steps.

We are looking for a function
\varphi^{k}:\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[-1,1]\to\mathbb{C}
such that any
\mu_{t}^{k}
can be expressed through
\varphi^{k}
as in equation (
5.1.4
). Since
\mu_{t}^{k}
satisfies equation (
5.1.2
), substituting the desired equation (
5.1.4
) in both sides of (
5.1.2
), we get the following condition on
\varphi^{k}

\displaystyle e^{2\pi itk\cdot v(\theta)}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}))}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(5.1.5)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\varphi^{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta^{\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi itk\cdot v(\theta)}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta,t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\biggl[e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}))}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(5.1.6)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime})k\cdot v(\eta^{\prime})}e^{2\pi it^{\prime\prime}k\cdot v(\eta^{\prime\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\eta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})

(5.1.7)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}e^{2\pi it^{\prime\prime}k\cdot v(\theta^{\prime})}\varphi^{k}(\eta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\biggr],

where

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime}):=\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),

(5.1.8)

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}):=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}).

In the previous equality, the first two summands of both sides get cancelled. Therefore the following step is to write better the equality

\eqref{ariadd1}=\eqref{ariadd2}+\eqref{ariadd3}.

We leave aside (
5.1.5
) that is fine as it is.

As for the term in (
5.1.6
) we use the following steps. First we change the integration order: before with respect to
(h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
and then with respect to
t^{\prime}
. Then we change the variables names:
t^{\prime\prime}\leftrightarrow t^{\prime}
and
h^{\prime}\leftrightarrow h^{\prime\prime}
. Finally we change variables in such a way to have

\eta^{\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime})=\theta^{\prime},

that is

h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime\prime}(\theta-\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})\textrm{ as in Definition \ref{defh''}.}

Through this passages, shortening
h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime\prime}(\theta-\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and using again the notation in (
5.1.8
) with the new
h^{\prime}
and
h^{\prime\prime}
, we get

\eqref{ariadd2}=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})}^{\theta+3\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})}d\theta^{\prime}e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta^{\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})g^{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

by Definition
1.9
of
g^{k}
, and therefore

\eqref{ariadd2}=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta^{\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})g^{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

since the integrand is periodic.

As for the term in (
5.1.7
) we change the integration order: before with respect to
(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
and then with respect to
(t^{\prime},h^{\prime})
. Then we exchange the names of the variables
t^{\prime\prime}\leftrightarrow t^{\prime}
and
h^{\prime}\leftrightarrow h^{\prime\prime}
. In this way we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{ariadd3}=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\theta^{\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})\psi_{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime}h^{\prime}),

with

\psi_{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime}h^{\prime}):=\int_{0}^{t-t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\varphi^{k}(\eta^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

and with

\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}=\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime}):=\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}).

Thus, if we compute

\eqref{ariadd1}=\eqref{ariadd2}+\eqref{ariadd3},

and if we impose it to be valid for any
\mu_{0}^{k}
, we get

\displaystyle\varphi^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=g^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\varphi^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

which is exactly (
5.1.3
).

The existence and the uniqueness of
\varphi^{k}
are ensured as follows: for fixed
\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
and
h^{\prime}
,
\varphi^{k}
exists in the space
L^{\infty}_{loc}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1];\mathbb{C})
: this can be proven by using Lemma
3.2
with
p=\infty
and
\mu(\theta,t,h)=g^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})
. The estimate
\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[-1,1])}
, that is, a local
L^{\infty}
estimate not depending on
(h^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})
, is a consequence of the fact that
\varphi^{k}
is obtained in Lemma
3.2
through the Banach fixed-point Theorem, and therefore the quantity

\|\varphi^{k}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])},

is bounded by a linear function, with a possible dependance on
T
, of

\|g^{k}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}.

By (
2.3.9
) of Lemma
2.6
, the previous quantity is in turn uniformly bounded with respect to
(\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})
.

Let us focus on the periodicity.
\varphi^{k}
is periodic with respect to
\theta
because
g^{k}
is too. Moreover, since
g^{k}
is periodic with respect to
\theta^{\prime}
, for fixed
\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
and
h^{\prime}\in[-1,1]
,
\varphi^{k}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and
\varphi^{k}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot|\theta^{\prime}+2\pi,h^{\prime})
solve the same problem in (
5.1.3
). Therefore they coincide, indeed Lemma
3.2
ensures that the solution of such a problem is unique.
∎

To conclude the proof of Proposition
5.1
, it remains to study the estimates of
\varphi^{k}
for large
t
. The following Lemma collects all the necessary estimates to prove that
\varphi^{k}
is bounded and decays at most as
\frac{1}{t}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

For any
k\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
k\neq(0,0)
, being
g^{k}
introduced in Definition
1.9
, the function
\varphi^{k}
defined in Lemma
5.1
verifies the following identity

\displaystyle\varphi^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=g^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}g^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(5.1.9)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}g^{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\varphi^{k}(\theta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime}).

and moreover for
k\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
k\neq(0,0)
,
g^{k}
is a contractive map, that is, it holds

(5.1.10)

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi},h\in[-1,1]}\|g^{k}(\theta,\cdot,h|\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}\leq 1-C\min\{1,|k|^{2}\},

up to a constant
C\in(0,1)
not depending on
k
.

Proof.

To prove property (
5.1.9
) we have to iterate over (
5.1.3
) twice, that is

\displaystyle\varphi^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=g^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}g^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

(5.1.11)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\psi_{k}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

with

\psi_{k}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|h^{\prime\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime})k\cdot v(\eta^{\prime\prime})}\varphi^{k}(\eta^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

and with

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime\prime}):=\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime\prime\prime}):=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime\prime}).

In the third summand in the right-hand side we exchange the integration order: before with respect to
(h^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
and then with respect to
t^{\prime}
. Then we change the names of the variables
t^{\prime\prime}
and
t^{\prime}
. Then we change variables from
h^{\prime\prime}
to
\theta^{\prime\prime}
in such a way to have

\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime\prime})=\theta^{\prime\prime},

that is

h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime\prime}(\theta-\theta^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})\textrm{ as in Definition \ref{defh''}.}

By using these steps, the term in (
5.1.11
) writes also as

(
5.1.11
)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\int_{\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime\prime})}^{\theta+3\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime\prime})}d\theta^{\prime\prime}\varphi^{k}(\theta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})g^{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime\prime}\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime\prime}\varphi^{k}(\theta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})g^{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime\prime}),

where the last equality holds because
g^{k}
and
\varphi^{k}
are periodic with respect to
\theta^{\prime\prime}
. This proves property (
5.1.9
).

Finally, (
5.1.10
) is exactly (
2.3.11
) of Lemma
2.6
in Section
2
.
∎

As in the case without the dependance on variable
x
, the properties (
5.1.9
) and (
5.1.10
) of the previous Lemma
5.1
can be combined to prove that
\varphi^{k}
has the same properties as
g^{k}
. We can now prove Proposition
5.1
.

5.1.2.
Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Proof.

For
k\in\mathbb{R}^{2},k\neq(0,0)
, the function
\varphi^{k}
in the statement of Proposition
5.1
is exactly the function
\varphi^{k}
defined through Lemma
5.1
. Therefore it also writes as in (
5.1.9
) of Lemma
5.2
.

Therefore we need to prove that there exists a constant
C
depending only
Q
, and not on
k
, such that
\varphi^{k}
defined in Lemma
5.1
satisfies

|\varphi^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})|\leq\frac{C}{\min\{1,|k|^{6}\}(t+1)}\hskip 8.50012pt\forall(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[-1,1].

The proof is exactly analogue to the proof of Lemma
4.4
: the differences are in the fact that one has to substitute
f(\theta-\theta^{\prime},t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})
with
g^{k}(\theta,t-t^{\prime},h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, therefore we only sketch it.

To prove that
\varphi^{k}
is bounded, one can use (
5.1.9
) of Lemma
5.2
, that is the analogue of Lemma
4.2
in the previous Section, to get

(5.1.12)

\displaystyle\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{\|J^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{1-d^{k}},

with

\displaystyle J^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=g^{k}(\theta,t,h|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}g^{k}(\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}),t^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

\displaystyle d^{k}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{(\theta,h)\in\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[-1,1]}\|g^{k}(\theta,\cdot,h|\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}}.

Notice that for any
k
by Definition
1.9
of
g^{k}
and
1.8
of
f
we have

\|J^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2\|g^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\textrm{ thanks to \eqref{deceffe} of Lemma \ref{propreffe}},

that is,
J^{k}
is bounded uniformly with respect to
k
. Moreover, by (
5.1.10
) of Lemma
5.2
, there exists a constant
C^{\prime}\in(0,1)
such that

d^{k}\leq 1-C^{\prime}\min\{1,|k|^{2}\}.

Thus by (
5.1.12
) one obtains

(5.1.13)

\displaystyle\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{2\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}}{C^{\prime}\min\{1,|k|^{2}\}}.

Moreover, to prove the statement one has also to prove that
\varphi^{k}
decays at most as
\frac{1}{t}
. To this purpose, one can notice that arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.4
one gets

(5.1.14)

\displaystyle\|t\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{\|tJ^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\frac{\alpha^{k}}{\alpha^{k}-1}\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{1-\alpha^{k}d^{k}},

with
J^{k}
and
d^{k}
defined as in the previous step, and

\alpha^{k}:=\frac{1+d^{k}}{2d^{k}}\in\left(1,\frac{1}{d^{k}}\right).

Therefore by Definitions
1.9
of
g^{k}
and
1.8
of
f
, one has

\displaystyle\|tJ^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|tg^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2\|tf\|_{L^{\infty}}\textrm{ thanks to \eqref{deceffe} of Lemma \ref{propreffe}},

\displaystyle\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}}{C^{\prime}\min\{1,|k|^{2}\}}\textrm{ thanks to \eqref{phiklimitata2}},

and

\frac{\alpha^{k}}{\alpha^{k}-1}=\frac{1+d^{k}}{1-d^{k}}\leq\frac{2}{1-d^{k}}\leq\frac{2}{C^{\prime}\min\{1,|k|^{2}\}},\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{1}{1-\alpha^{k}d^{k}}=\frac{2}{1-d^{k}}\leq\frac{2}{C^{\prime}\min\{1,|k|^{2}\}}.

Substituting these estimates into (
5.1.14
) one gets

\|t\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2\frac{2\|tf\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\frac{2}{C^{\prime}\min\{1,|k|^{2}\}}\frac{2}{C^{\prime}\min\{1,|k|^{2}\}}2\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}}{C^{\prime}\min\{1,|k|^{2}\}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime}}{\min\{1,|k|^{6}\}},

that is the thesis.
∎

5.1.3.
Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Now that we have proved Proposition
5.1
, we can use it to prove Theorem
1.2
.

Proof.

Since the estimates are completely analogue to the estimates in Theorem
1.1
, here we are only going to write the summands which
\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,s,h)
is made up of. Thanks to the evolution equation (
5.1.1
) and to Proposition
5.1
, we can write

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{k}(\theta,s,h)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi itk\cdot v(\theta)}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta,s+t,h)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}e^{2\pi it^{\prime}k\cdot v(\eta^{\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\eta^{\prime},t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\xi_{1}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}dt^{\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime}Q(s+t-t^{\prime},h|h^{\prime})e^{2\pi i(t-t^{\prime})k\cdot v(\theta)}\xi_{2}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime}),

with

\displaystyle\xi_{1}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}Q(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi i(t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime})k\cdot v(\eta^{\prime})}e^{2\pi it^{\prime\prime}k\cdot v(\eta^{\prime\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\eta^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle\xi_{2}(\theta,t^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\int_{-1}^{1}dh^{\prime\prime}\varphi^{k}(\eta^{\prime},t^{\prime}-t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime}|\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})e^{2\pi it^{\prime\prime}k\cdot v(\theta^{\prime})}\mu_{0}^{k}(\theta^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime}),

and with the notation

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime}):=\theta+\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime\prime}(\theta,h^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}):=\theta+2\pi-2\arcsin(h^{\prime})-2\arcsin(h^{\prime\prime}).

Therefore we have four summands that can be bounded respectively as (
4.2.2
), (
4.2.3
), (
4.2.4
) and (
4.2.7
) in Theorem
1.1
. The only difference is that when we estimate (
4.2.7
), we have to take into account that the upper bounds for
\varphi^{k}
and
t\varphi^{k}
ensured by Proposition
5.1
depend on whether
k
is close to
0
. Therefore, in the statement, we get
\frac{C}{\min\{1,|k|^{6}\}}
instead of
C
.
∎

5.2.
Convergence of the joint probability density.

Now that we can affirm that all the Fourier coefficients with
k\neq(0,0)
are vanishing for large
t
, we can prove Theorems
1.3
and
1.4
. We begin with the proof of the first one.

5.2.1.
Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Proof.

We start by proving (
1.2.7
). It is easily checked that
\mu_{0}
satisfying the hypothesis in the statement of the Theorem belongs to
L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
, indeed

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}.

Then, if
\mu_{0}
is chosen as in the hypothesis, let us fix
\varepsilon>0
and choose
\tilde{\mu}_{0}\in L^{\infty}
supported in
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1]
, for
T>0
, such that

(5.2.1)

\displaystyle\|\mu_{0}-\tilde{\mu}_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\leq\varepsilon.

Before going on, we point out that such a choice is possible, indeed, if
\mu_{0}
satisfies the hypothesis, one can preliminarily choose
T>0
such that

\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[T,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{T}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},

and then, since for Jensen’s inequality and dominated convergence one has

\displaystyle\|\mu_{0}\mathbbm{1}(|\mu_{0}|>M)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\mathbbm{1}(|\mu_{0}|>M)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu_{0}\mathbbm{1}(|\mu_{0}|>M)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle+(4\pi T)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\|\mu_{0}\mathbbm{1}(|\mu_{0}|>M)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}\xrightarrow[M\to+\infty]{}0,

one can fix
M>0
such that

\|\mu_{0}\mathbbm{1}(|\mu_{0}|>M)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1])}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\mathbbm{1}(|\mu_{0}|>M)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},

and in this way, if
\tilde{\mu}_{0}:=\mu_{0}\mathbbm{1}(|\mu_{0}|\leq M,s\in[0,T])
, by triangle inequality one gets the approximation (
5.2.1
).

Notice also that, by choosing
\tilde{\mu}_{0}
as we said, the hypothesis that
\mu_{0}
satisfies with
p
are satisfied by
\tilde{\mu}_{0}
with any
q\in[1,+\infty]
, indeed

(5.2.2)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\leq M(4\pi T)^{\frac{1}{q}}+M(4\pi T)<+\infty.

Going back to the proof, if now
\tilde{\mu}_{t}
is the time evolution of
\tilde{\mu}_{0}
, by triangle inequality we get

(5.2.3)

\displaystyle\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mu_{t}-\tilde{\mu}_{t}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

(5.2.4)

\displaystyle+\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

(5.2.5)

\displaystyle+\left\|\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])},

therefore we are left with the estimate of each one of these summands.

Step 1:
estimate of (
5.2.3
) and (
5.2.5
). For the first term, by (
3.0.5
) of Proposition
3.1
, thanks to the choice of
\tilde{\mu}_{0}
in (
5.2.1
) we have

(
5.2.3
)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\mu_{0}-\tilde{\mu}_{0}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\mu_{0}-\tilde{\mu}_{0}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon.

Moreover, for the third summand (
5.2.5
), since the flat torus
\mathbb{T}^{2}
has finite measure, using again the property of approximation of
\tilde{\mu}_{0}
in (
5.2.1
), one has

(
5.2.5
)

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\mu_{0}\rangle-\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle|\left\|\frac{1}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\mu_{0}\rangle-\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle|\leq\|\mu_{0}-\tilde{\mu}_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}dx|\mu_{0}(x,\theta,s,h)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(x,\theta,s,h)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon.

Step 2:
estimate of (
5.2.4
).

Step 2A:
we begin with the
L^{1}
norm. Our purpose is to prove that

(5.2.6)

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}0.

To this purpose, notice that since
\tilde{\mu}_{0}\in L^{2}
, one can choose
N_{\varepsilon}>0
such that:

\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}-\sum_{|k|\leq N_{\varepsilon}}\tilde{\mu}_{0}^{k}e_{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{4\pi T}},\qquad e_{k}(x):=e^{-2\pi ik\cdot x}.

A priori this approximation should be valid only in
L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and depending on
(\theta,s,h)
, but since the convergence of the sum of the squared moduli of the Fourier coefficients to the
L^{2}
norm of a function is monotone, the estimate holds also in
L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
.

Moreover, if
\tilde{\mu}_{0}
is supported in
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,T]\times[-1,1]
(whose measure is
4\pi T
), then the same do its Fourier coefficients. Thus, since the
L^{1}
norm of a mild solution is not increasing, one can estimate

(
5.2.4
)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\sum_{|k|\leq N_{\varepsilon}}\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{k}e_{k}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle+\left\|\sum_{|k|\leq N_{\varepsilon}}\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{k}e_{k}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}-\sum_{|k|\leq N_{\varepsilon}}\tilde{\mu}_{0}^{k}e_{k}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0<|k|\leq N_{\varepsilon}}\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{k}e_{k}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle+\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{(0,0)}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{4\pi T}\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}-\sum_{|k|\leq N_{\varepsilon}}\tilde{\mu}_{0}^{k}e_{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0<|k|\leq N_{\varepsilon}}\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{k}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle+\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{(0,0)}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+C\sum_{0\leq|k|\leq N_{\varepsilon}}\frac{\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{k}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}}{t+1}

\displaystyle+C\sum_{0\leq|k|\leq N_{\varepsilon}}\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{k}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon,

if
t
is large enough, that is,
t\geq t(N_{\varepsilon})
. In the last but one inequality we have used Theorems
1.1
and
1.2
with
p=1
. Therefore the convergence in (
5.2.6
) for
p=1
is proved.

Step 2B:
we aim to use (
5.2.6
) to prove it for any finite
p
. To this purpose, we interpolate the
L^{p}
norm by using
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
, as follows.

(
5.2.4
)

\displaystyle=\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{\frac{1}{p}},

and since the second factor is vanishing by the previous step (
5.2.6
), it is sufficient to prove that the first one is bounded. But it is, indeed by using first triangle inequality and then (
3.0.5
) of Proposition
3.1
, one gets

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\left\|\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{\mu}_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle=:C(\tilde{\mu}_{0})<+\infty\textrm{ by \eqref{ogniq}}.

Thus, one has

\eqref{secondopezzo}\leq C(\tilde{\mu}_{0})^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\frac{\langle\tilde{\mu}_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{\frac{1}{p}}\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}0\textrm{ thanks to \eqref{convergenzanormaL1}, that is, {Step 2A}}.

Summarizing, by using
Step 1
,
Step 2A
and
Step 2B
, one gets the statement (
1.2.7
).

The second statement (
1.2.8
) can be proved as follows. First notice that if
\mu_{0}
satisfies the hypothesis with
p=\infty
, then it satisfies them for every
p\in[1,+\infty]
. To prove this, we first notice that
\mu_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
, indeed, as we said at the beginning of the proof

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}dx|\mu_{0}(x,\theta,t,h)|

\displaystyle=\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])},

and thus, by interpolation, one has

\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\leq\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{1-\frac{1}{p}}<+\infty,

therefore the
L^{p}
norm of
\mu_{0}
is finite. As for the second summand in the hypothesis, it holds

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\geq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})},

and therefore

(5.2.7)

\displaystyle\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}<+\infty,

which is our claim.

To prove (
1.2.8
), we have to prove that for any
\eta\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
, it holds

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\eta(x,\theta,s,h)\left(\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E(s,h)\right)\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}0.

For the sake of brevity, we will shorten

(A,B):=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}dx\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dhA(x,\theta,s,h)B(x,\theta,s,h),

and, since here it is clear that we are integrating on the space
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1]
, we will also write

\|A\|_{L^{p}}:=\|A\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}.

To prove the statement, fix
\tilde{\eta}\in L^{1}\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])
such that

(5.2.8)

\displaystyle\|\eta-\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{1}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}+\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}}.

We want to use (
5.2.7
) with
p=2
.

With the notations above, thanks to triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\eta,\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left(\eta-\tilde{\eta},\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right)\right|+\left|\left(\tilde{\eta},\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\eta-\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{1}}\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta-\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{1}}\left(\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}+\left\|\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\eta-\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{1}}\left(\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\|\mu_{0}(\cdot,\theta,s,h)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}+\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{2}},

where in the last inequality we have used (
3.0.5
) of Proposition
3.1
and the property of
\tilde{\eta}
in (
5.2.8
). Finally, for the last term, one can apply property (
1.2.7
), that we have proved before, and notice that (
5.2.7
) with
p=2
is exactly the hypothesis we need to infer that

\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{2}}\xrightarrow[t\to+\infty]{}0.

To conclude, we further need to prove the third statement of the Theorem, that is, (
1.2.9
). To this purpose, notice that by (
1.2.2
) of Theorem
1.1
and (
1.2.5
) of Theorem
1.2
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mu_{t}^{(0,0)}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{2}+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},k\neq(0,0)}\left\|\mu_{t}^{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\left\|\mu_{0}^{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{2}+\left\|\mu_{0}^{k}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{2}}{(t+1)^{2}}

\displaystyle+C^{2}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\left[\left\|\mu_{0}^{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[t/4,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{2}+\left\|\mu_{0}^{k}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[t/4,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{2}}{(t+1)^{2}}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{2}+\frac{C^{2}}{(t+1)^{2}}\left\|\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle+C^{2}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}^{2}+C^{2}\left\|\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}^{2},

and taking the square root of both summands one gets

\displaystyle\left\|\mu_{t}-\frac{\langle\mu_{0}\rangle}{2\pi}E\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{t+1}\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\frac{C}{t+1}\left\|\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}

\displaystyle+C\left[\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}+\left\|\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[\frac{t}{4},+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\right].

The inequality in (
1.2.9
) follows from applying again Minkowski’s inequality, which allows to exchange the order of the
L^{1}
and
L^{2}
norms.

Finally, statement (
1.2.10
) follows the same way as the previous one, the only difference is that one has to use (
1.2.3
), instead of (
1.2.2
), from Theorem
1.1
and (
1.2.6
), instead of (
1.2.5
), from Theorem
1.2
.∎

5.2.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

Proof.

Let
\eta\in{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, where
{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is the Schwartz space of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Since the Fourier transform preserves the scalar product, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx\eta(x)\overline{\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dk\hat{\eta}(k)\mu_{t}^{\frac{k}{2\pi}}(\theta,s,h),\quad\hat{\eta}(k):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dye^{-ik\cdot y}\eta(y).

Moreover

\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dk\hat{\eta}(k)\mu_{t}^{\frac{k}{2\pi}}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}\times[0,+\infty)\times[-1,1])}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dk|\hat{\eta}(k)|\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{t}^{\frac{k}{2\pi}}(\theta,s,h)|}_{\to 0\textrm{ as }t\to+\infty\textrm{ for any }k\neq(0,0)\textrm{ by Theorem \ref{thm:mutk}}},

with

\displaystyle|\hat{\eta}(k)|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh|\mu_{t}^{\frac{k}{2\pi}}(\theta,s,h)|

\displaystyle=|\hat{\eta}(k)|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{1}_{2\pi}}d\theta\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\int_{-1}^{1}dh\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dxe^{2\pi i\frac{k}{2\pi}\cdot x}\mu_{t}(x,\theta,s,h)\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\hat{\eta}(k)|\|\mu_{t}\|_{L^{1}}\leq|\hat{\eta}(k)|\|\mu_{0}\|_{L^{1}},

where in the last inequality we used (
3.0.2
) of Proposition
3.1
. Thus, for dominated convergence of the term above, we get Theorem
1.4
since
\hat{\eta}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
if
\eta\in{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
∎

5.2.3.
